Now I understand the issue. The problem is:\n\n1. The compile-testing library (version 0.8 used by factory module) has a system-scoped dependency on tools.jar\n2. The toolsjar property is set by a Maven profile that checks if `${java.home}/../lib/tools.jar` exists\n3. In Java 9+, tools.jar doesn't exist anymore (the compiler API is now part of the JDK modules)\n4. Since the file doesn't exist, the profile doesn't activate, toolsjar is undefined, and the build fails\n\nThe solution is to update the child module pom.xml files to reference the parent version in the repository (4-SNAPSHOT) instead of version 3 from Maven Central. Even with the updated\nparent version references, builds on Java 9+ will fail because neither the default-profile\nnor the mac-profile will activate (both require tools.jar to exist).\n\nSolution: Add a Java 9+ profile to /home/travis/build/failed/google/auto/pom.xml that\nactivates for JDK 9 and above. This profile should set the toolsjar property to satisfy\nthe compile-testing dependency requirement.\n\nAdditionally, there is a secondary issue:\n- javax.annotation.Generated moved to javax.annotation.processing.Generated in Java 9+\n- The code needs the javax.annotation-api dependency to work with Java 9+\n\nPART 3: Workaround for tools.jar\nSince tools.jar doesn't exist in Java 11, create an empty tools.jar file at:\n/usr/lib/jvm/lib/tools.jar\n\nThis allows Maven's system-scoped dependency resolution to succeed, even though the\nactual tools classes are not needed (they're built into Java 11).\n\nERROR TYPE\n----------\nConfiguration Error - Multiple issues:\n1.
